# Phase 3 — Editing depth

See [PLAN.md](PLAN.md#phase-3--editing-depth) for the whole phase and [USABILITY.md](USABILITY.md) for the decisions behind it. Phase 3 adds darktable's editing tools behind Lightroom-style controls. It starts with the groundwork they need: process versions, so that rendering changes never alter existing edits, and the Develop tool stack that the new tools join, with Crop as its first group.

## Part 1: Process versions, the tool stack, and Crop

**What it does.** Three things. Every edit now records which version of LightBench's rendering it uses (its **process version**), so later rendering changes never silently change an existing edit. Develop's right panel is now **one scrolling stack of tool groups** in workflow order with a **jump bar**. Its new first group is **Crop**: crop, straighten, rotate 90° and flip, on the photo and in the panel, Lightroom-style.

Owner's choices (2026-10-12):
1. **Process versions first.** Existing edits are version 1 (today's rendering); new edits get the current version; rendering code can branch on the version; the version is shown unobtrusively and an older edit offers **Update to current process**, which can be compared and undone through the history. Today there is one rendering, so this is the mechanism and its tests.
2. **The tool stack**, already decided after round 2: one scrolling stack, Crop & Lens first, then Basic; collapsible groups; a jump bar with icons and one-word labels (labels hide when narrow, the name shows on hover). Only groups with content exist (Crop, Basic); Curve, Colour, Detail and Effects slot in later. The on/off **eye** per group (an idea from LightCraft; LightBench's choice to include it) shows the photo without that group.
3. **Crop, Lightroom-style:** **R** opens it on the photo (an action in the shortcut registry, so it can be changed); drag corners or edges to resize, inside to move, outside to turn; the crop box stays level and the photo turns under it (Lightroom's way; LightCraft turns the box instead). Aspects Original, 1:1, 4:5, 5:7, 2:3, 16:9 and Custom, with a lock; **X** swaps portrait and landscape; **O** cycles thirds, grid, golden ratio and diagonals while cropping; **Enter** applies, **Esc** cancels; a Reset.
4. **Straighten:** an Angle slider (−45…+45°, 0.01° steps; the crop shrinks to stay inside the photo) and a level line drawn along the horizon or a wall (the **Level** button, or **Ctrl-drag** while cropping, as in LightCraft (and, from general knowledge, Lightroom; not checked here)). **Rotate left/right** (Ctrl+[ / Ctrl+]) and **flip** horizontal/vertical. Auto straighten comes later with Upright.

### Process versions

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| What is stored | `process` in the develop settings (`engine/develop/settings.ts`), saved in the catalog, the history and the `lightbench:Develop` sidecar field. It is separate from the settings' existing `version: 1`, which numbers the JSON layout (the schema) and stays as it was. | Owner's decision; design LightBench's |
| Old and new edits | A saved edit without `process` (everything saved before 0.3.0) reads as version 1. New edits, unedited photos and **Reset all** get the current version. A malformed value reads as 1. | Owner's decision |
| What a version covers | Everything that turns the same settings into different pixels: the RAW stage (white level choice, highlight reconstruction, demosaic), colour (camera matrix, white balance maths), the slider mappings (Exposure, the Contrast → sigmoid mapping, the tone-equalizer bands behind Highlights/Shadows/Whites/Blacks, Vibrance/Saturation), the display transform, and the geometry stage (crop rounding and the warp kernel). A change that would alter a saved edit's pixels needs a new version; fixes for broken output and new tools that are off by default don't. Written down in `engine/develop/process.ts`. | LightBench |
| Branching on it | Each version has its own parameter set (`processParams(version)`): today the tone range, the tone-equalizer band weights and the contrast spread, moved out of fixed constants. `displayUniforms` and `toneBands` read them from the photo's version. A future version adds an entry; version 1's entry never changes. | LightBench |
| Shown | At the foot of the tool stack: “Process version 1 (current)”. An older edit also shows, above the groups, “This photo was edited with process version N” and **Update to current process**, which records one history step “Update to process M”: the step before it shows the old rendering, Ctrl+Z goes back. An edit from a newer LightBench says so and is shown with the newest version this build knows, never rewritten. | Lightroom's process version and its update prompt (from general knowledge of Lightroom; not checked in Lightroom here) |
| Copy/paste | **Process version** is an item of the paste checklist's Advanced group (unticked by default, like the rest of that group). | Lightroom's Copy Settings lists Process Version (not checked here) |
| Testing an update | When this part was written only one rendering existed, so no real edit was older than current; version 2 came with [Part 10](#part-10-the-tone-equalizer-as-darktables-defaults-process-version-2). On the development server, `?simulateProcess=N` (N above the newest version) adds a copy of the newest version as version N so the offer can be tried; production builds leave it out (`import.meta.env.DEV`). | Test scaffolding |

### The tool stack

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Stack | Develop's right panel is one scrolling list: **Crop**, then **Basic** (the existing White balance, Tone, Presence and Advanced sections, unchanged). Each group has a heading with its icon; clicking it closes or opens the group (remembered in this browser). | Owner's decision (USABILITY.md, “Order of groups”) |
| Jump bar | Stays at the top of the panel. One button per group, an icon and a one-word label (Crop, Basic); the button of the group in view is highlighted as you scroll. Clicking scrolls that group's heading to the top, opening it if it was closed. **R** also brings the Crop group into view. | Owner's decision |
| Narrow panel | Labels hide when the buttons don't fit with them (measured from the labels' own width); each button keeps its name as a tooltip. With two groups this only happens far below the panel's minimum width; with all six planned groups (Crop, Basic, Curve, Colour, Detail, Effects) it would start at roughly 400 px (an estimate from the label lengths). | Owner's decision |
| Later groups | `ToolStack.tsx` takes a list of groups; Curve, Colour, Detail and Effects are planned entries (`GroupId`) and appear when they have content. | Owner's decision |
| On/off eye | The eye in a group's heading shows the photo (and the histogram) without that group, for a before/after of just that group; nothing is saved and the history doesn't change. Crop's eye shows the whole photo (it appears once the photo is cropped, turned or flipped); Basic's shows white balance As Shot and the tone and presence sliders at zero (Advanced stays, so the RAW isn't reprocessed). A “Hidden: …” badge says what is off. | LightCraft's idea; LightBench's choice to include it |
| Hints | The Hints checkbox (in White balance) now applies to the whole stack, including the Angle slider. | As before |

### Crop, straighten, rotate and flip

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Open | **R**, the **Crop** button in the toolbar, or **Crop…** in the Crop group. The whole photo is shown turned by the straighten angle, with the crop box over it, the area outside dimmed and a 28 px margin for the handles. Zoom is fit while cropping. | Lightroom (R = Crop Overlay); key in the registry |
| Resize and move | Drag a corner or an edge (the opposite corner or edge stays), or drag inside to move. The box stops at the photo's edge and slides along it. With the aspect locked, corners and edges keep the shape (an edge grows about its middle). Smallest crop: 2 % of the short side. | LightCraft `crop_drag.rs`, ported |
| Turn | Drag outside the box: the photo turns under the level box (a grid and the angle show while dragging). The crop is computed from where the drag started, so turning back within one drag gives the crop back. | Lightroom (box level, photo turns); LightCraft turns the box instead, not copied |
| Angle slider | −45…+45° in 0.01° steps (arrow keys 0.45°, Shift+arrows 4.5°; double-click or Home resets). Moves continue from the crop before the slider started moving. | Owner's decision; step sizes from the slider keys (Part 12) |
| Crop stays inside | When the angle changes, a crop that still fits is kept. Otherwise it shrinks about its own centre, keeping its aspect, until it fits; if its centre is outside the turned photo, it becomes the largest crop of its aspect, centred. | LightCraft `with_angle` (largest centred crop); shrinking about the crop's own centre is LightBench's change, so the box doesn't jump |
| Level line | **Level**, or **Ctrl-drag** (⌘ on a Mac) while cropping: draw along something that should be level or upright. Lines closer to horizontal become horizontal, closer to vertical become upright; the photo turns by the line's tilt (if the result stays within ±45°). | Lightroom's Straighten tool; LightCraft also uses ⌘/Ctrl-drag |
| Aspect | Original, 1:1, 4:5, 5:7, 2:3, 16:9, Custom (width : height). Picking one locks it and makes the largest crop of that shape at the current angle, centred on the crop. The menu shows ratios in the crop's shape (3 : 2 for a landscape crop, 2 : 3 for a portrait one). The lock starts on, at Original. Locking a free crop keeps its shape (named if it matches a menu entry, else Custom). | Owner's list; default locked to Original is LightBench's choice (Lightroom's default not checked) |
| Swap | **X** (or the button): portrait ⇄ landscape about the crop's centre, shrunk to fit if needed. | Lightroom's X (not checked here) |
| Overlays | Thirds, Grid (8 × 8), Golden ratio (lines at 0.382 and 0.618), Diagonals (45° from each corner). **O** cycles them while cropping; outside the crop tool O still shows clipping, because the shortcut registry gives cropping its own context. The choice is remembered in this browser. | Owner's list; Lightroom's O cycles crop overlays (not checked here) |
| Apply, cancel, undo | **Enter**, R again, or **Done** apply the crop as one history step (“Crop”, or “Straighten” when only the angle changed). **Esc** or **Cancel** drop it. While cropping, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y undo and redo crop changes. Leaving the photo (filmstrip, another module) applies it (not tested end to end; Lightroom's behaviour here not checked). | Owner's decision |
| Reset | Puts crop, straighten, rotate and flip back (one history step outside the crop tool). | Owner's decision |
| Rotate and flip | **Ctrl+[** / **Ctrl+]** turn left/right (in Develop, also while cropping); Flip horizontal/vertical in the Crop group and in command search (no key by default). The crop turns and mirrors with the photo, so it stays on the same content; a flip mirrors the straighten angle too. Outside the crop tool each is a history step. These are part of the edit (on top of the camera's orientation), unlike Lightroom, where rotating changes the photo's orientation metadata. | Owner's keys (Lightroom's Ctrl+[ / Ctrl+]); storing them in the edit is LightBench's choice |
| Size | The crop's size in pixels (what Export writes at 100 %) is shown in the group. | LightBench |

### The geometry stage and where the crop is used

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| One resample | Lens correction → rotate and perspective (straighten; later Upright) → flip (camera orientation + the user's turns and flips) → crop, darktable's pixelpipe order, are combined into one projective map from output pixels to the sensor (`engine/develop/geometry.ts`, `resolveGeometry`). Lens correction and perspective are identity for now and join the same map later, so a photo is resampled once whatever tools are used. | darktable `src/common/iop_order.c` (v5.0 order: lens 13, ashift 15, flip 16, crop 24.5); combining them is LightBench's choice (darktable's modules each resample) |
| Interpolation | With a straighten angle, the scene is warped once with bicubic interpolation (Catmull-Rom, a = −0.5) into a cached texture with its own mip chain (`engine/gpu/warp.wgsl`). Without an angle the map is whole-pixel (orientation, turns, flips, crop) and the scene is read directly: nothing is resampled. | darktable's default warp interpolator is bicubic: `plugins/lighttable/export/pixel_interpolator_warp` defaults to `bicubic` (`data/darktableconfig.xml.in`) and `DT_INTERPOLATION_DEFAULT_WARP = DT_INTERPOLATION_BICUBIC` (`src/common/interpolation.h`); its `_maketaps_bicubic` is the a = −0.5 cubic (`src/common/interpolation.c`) |
| Cache | The last warped scene is kept per scene and map: exposure, tone and colour sliders, Auto, the histogram, thumbnails and exports reuse it. Before/after and the eyes (which show no geometry) don't evict it. A new RAW stage (white balance, highlight reconstruction, demosaic) or another photo drops it. | LightCraft review (“cache the warped result”) |
| Crop coordinates | Normalised (0…1) in the straightened frame: the photo after every geometry correction except the crop, in its final orientation. So crops (and later masks) stay on the same content when lens or Upright settings change. | Owner's requirement; LightCraft's model |
| Whole pixels | The crop is rounded inwards to whole pixels, so a turned crop never reads outside the photo. A locked aspect gets the whole-pixel size nearest to it (a 1:1 crop is exactly square). A crop found outside the turned photo (e.g. pasted where the photo's size wasn't known) is pulled inside before rendering. | LightBench |
| Everywhere | Develop's view, the histogram and clipping, **Auto** (it measures the cropped frame), Export (`renderOutput`: the crop at 100 %, then resized), thumbnails (`renderThumbnail`), the culling/Compare/Survey 100 % renders (`FullRenderer`), the background developer (Paste, Sync, Auto Sync, presets, develop new photos) and virtual copies all render through the same map, because the geometry travels in `displayUniforms`. The white balance picker and the sensor-clipping overlay map through it too. | Owner's requirement |
| Old edits | An edit without geometry renders exactly as before: the map is built in uv units from 0, 1 and −1 entries and equals the old orientation map (unit test), and the full-size export of a 0.2.0 edit is bit-identical (below). | Owner's requirement |

### Copy, paste and presets

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Checklist | A new **Crop** group in the Copy/Sync/Paste/New preset checklist: **Rotate & flip**, **Straighten angle**, **Crop** (rectangle, aspect and lock). Unticked by default, like the Advanced group. | Lightroom doesn't copy the crop unless it is ticked, as the owner described it; not checked in Lightroom here, so also LightBench's choice |
| Order | Pasted in that order: the orientation first (the crop is relative to it, and turning moves the crop with the photo), then the angle (the target's crop shrinks to fit), then the rectangle. | LightBench |
| Other shapes | A crop with a locked aspect keeps that aspect on a photo of other proportions (the largest rectangle of the ratio inside the pasted one, about its centre); a free crop is copied proportionally. The crop is then kept inside the target photo at its angle. | LightBench's choice |
| Presets and Amount | Presets can hold the crop items. The Amount slider treats geometry like the other switches: it follows the preset whenever the amount is above 0. | As Part 8 |
| Sidecars | The geometry is part of the settings in `lightbench:Develop`. Lightroom's `crs:Crop*` and `tiff:Orientation` are read and written too (Part 7). | Owner's decision |

### Shortcuts added

| Action | Where | LightBench and Lightroom Classic sets |
|---|---|---|
| Crop and straighten (again: apply) | Develop, also while cropping | R |
| Rotate left / right | Develop, also while cropping | Ctrl+[ / Ctrl+] |
| Flip horizontal / vertical, Straighten: draw a level line, Reset crop… | Develop, also while cropping | no key (command search) |
| Apply the crop / Cancel the crop | Develop: cropping | Enter / Esc |
| Next crop overlay | Develop: cropping | O |
| Swap portrait and landscape | Develop: cropping | X |
| Lock or unlock the aspect | Develop: cropping | no key |
| Undo / Redo | now also while cropping (crop changes) | Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+Shift+Z |

The registry has a new context, **Develop: cropping** (`crop`), active instead of Develop while the crop tool is open, so O, X, Esc and Enter don't clash with Show clipping, the Library's keys or Develop's Esc. Develop's G moved from `App.tsx` into DevelopView, so it isn't active while cropping either. The unit test that checks both sets for conflicts now also covers the cropping context. Lightroom's keys for these were not checked against Adobe's pages here (R, Ctrl+[ and Ctrl+] are the owner's).

**Verified** (headless Chromium 141 with software WebGPU (SwiftShader), A.NEF = Nikon D700 `philadelphia.nef`, 4284 × 2844; B.NEF = Nikon D80 `glarus.nef`, 3900 × 2613; C.JPG = `test-srgb.jpg`, 1600 × 1072; scratch scripts `e2e-crop` (37 of 37 checks passed), `e2e-crop-extra` (9 of 9), `e2e-crop-match` and `e2e-oldedit`):
- **Old edits unchanged** (`e2e-oldedit`): an edit made with the code before this part (the `lightbench/keyboard` branch, whose rendering code is identical to `main`'s; Exposure +0.5, Contrast +20, Shadows +10, Saturation −10 on A) was saved, its `A.xmp` kept, and the folder opened again with this part's code. Full-size exports of A (edited), B and C (unedited) had identical SHA-256 hashes before and after; 1024 px exports and 512 px thumbnails differed in 0 bytes; histogram statistics were identical (A: 43,520 pixels, mean luma 120.871); the Develop canvas screenshot (890 × 719) differed in 0 bytes.
- **Tool stack:** the jump bar showed Crop and Basic with icons and labels, Crop first and highlighted. **Basic** scrolled the stack to 325 px (the Basic heading at the top, under the 43 px bar) and highlighted Basic; **Crop** scrolled back to 0. A closed Basic group opened again from the jump bar. At a 119 px wide bar the labels hid and both buttons kept their names as tooltips; widening showed them again. The foot of the stack read “Process version 1 (current)”.
- **Crop tool** on A (4284 × 2844): R opened it, starting at 4284 × 2844 locked to the original aspect. A corner drag gave 3537 × 2348 (ratio 1.5064 against 1.5063); a locked edge drag 3126 × 2075 with the opposite edge in place; unlocked, a top-edge drag changed only the height (3126 × 1767). 1:1 gave 2844 × 2844 and stayed square under a corner drag (2515 × 2515). 2:3 then X: 4266 × 2844 → 1896 × 2844. O cycled thirds → grid → golden → diagonals → thirds and no clipping overlay appeared.
- **Straighten:** dragging outside the box turned the photo to +17.35° with the box an untransformed rectangle; the Angle slider (Shift+→ twice) added exactly 9.00°; a Ctrl-drag line tilted by atan(30/300) turned it from 26.35° to 20.64° (expected 20.64°); the Level tool on a near-vertical line from 20.64° to 24.45° (expected 24.45°).
- **Rotate and flip:** Ctrl+[ turned the 2730 × 1820 crop to 1820 × 2730 (“Turned left”), Ctrl+] back. Flip horizontal mirrored the angle (24.45° → −24.45°), both flips read “Turned 180°”, flipping back restored +24.45°. Ctrl+Z inside the crop tool undid the last flip. Esc left the photo uncropped (4284 × 2844) with the history unchanged.
- **Apply:** a 1:1 crop moved left and turned +3.15° became one history step “Crop” on Enter; Develop showed it square and fitted (photo found at x 86–803 for an expected 85.5–804.5).
- **Histogram and Auto use the crop:** statistics of the cropped frame: 65,536 pixels (256 × 256), mean luma 94.91, against 43,520 pixels and 104.09 for the whole photo; 2,654 pixels of the histogram drawing changed. Auto on the whole photo: Exposure +0.75, Blacks −85; on the crop: Exposure +1.20, Blacks −100.
- **Thumbnails:** A's filmstrip thumbnail became 512 × 512; after Paste, B's grid thumbnail too.
- **Export:** PNG at original size: 2634 × 2634, the crop's size. Compared with Develop at 100 % (`e2e-crop-match`, a 2698 × 2698 straightened crop, window sized so the photo sits on whole pixels): mean difference 0.34 levels, maximum 2 levels over 632,060 pixels. (Element screenshots start half a pixel high because the top bar is 55.5 px tall; the comparison is aligned for that row.) At fit the Develop view averages scene-linear pixels before developing them while the export averages after, so fine high-contrast detail differs slightly there with or without a crop: whole photo mean 2.24 levels (median 1, 99th percentile 24); straightened crop mean 3.86 (median 1, 99th 45).
- **Copy and paste:** in the Copy checklist, Crop and Straighten angle were unticked by default; Crop showed “62 × 93 % of the photo, 1:1”. Pasting onto B (3900 × 2613) gave a square 2398 × 2398 crop at +3.15°, and B.xmp held the geometry with `aspect` 1:1 and `process` 1.
- **More** (`e2e-crop-extra`): on C.JPG, Ctrl+] outside the crop tool turned 1600 × 1072 into 1072 × 1600 as a history step “Rotate right”; Develop showed it portrait; its thumbnail became 343 × 512; the culling/Compare/Survey 100 % renderer (`FullRenderer`) returned 1072 × 1600, and so did a virtual copy made afterwards. On A, choosing 1:1 outside the crop tool recorded “Crop aspect”, and `FullRenderer` returned 2844 × 2844. The Crop eye showed the whole photo full width with “Hidden: Crop” and then the square crop again, without a history step. With Exposure +2 EV, the Basic eye took the mean brightness from 182.2 to 124 and back to 182.2.
- **Process version** (development server with `?simulateProcess=2`): A's version-1 edit showed “This photo was edited with process version 1.” and **Update to current process**; clicking it gave “Process version 2 (current)” and the history step “Update to process 2”; Ctrl+Z went back to version 1 with the offer shown again. The production build contains no `simulateProcess` code.
- **Earlier scripts re-run, same results as before:** Part 7's paste script (A +0.75 EV / Blacks −85, B −0.20 / −4, C −0.20 / −5, Sync and Auto Sync reaching B.xmp, strength 50 % giving Blacks −42); Part 8's presets script (Punchy on new photos, Soft at 50 % giving Contrast +3, New preset ticking the seven changed settings, Auto tone at 50 % giving +0.38 EV / −42); Part 12's keyboard script, 89 of 89 checks.
- Screenshots of the crop tool (thirds overlay; turned, with the grid and angle while dragging and after), the tool stack and jump bar (top, scrolled to Basic, narrow), the Crop eye off and the process offer were checked by eye.
- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`, 257 passing): `crop.test.ts` (LightCraft's crop and drag tests, plus two 500-case randomised property tests: always inside the turned photo, the anchored edges stay, the locked ratio holds, a move keeps the size, no failure on hostile numbers; the largest inscribed crop against the closed form), `geometry.test.ts` (old edits read as no geometry; the map without a crop equals the old orientation map exactly for all four camera orientations; whole-pixel inward cropping; locked aspects exact in pixels; uv and pixel maps agree; a turned crop reads only inside the photo; rotate right/left and both flips move the content as expected, crop and angle included; four turns and two flips are identity; straighten shrink and limits; level lines; aspect pick, swap and lock; paste across shapes), `process.test.ts` (old edits are version 1 through every load path, new edits current, version 1's parameters and formulas exactly as before, unknown newer versions, ordering), `paste.test.ts` (crop unticked by default, locked aspect kept on another shape, rotate → angle → crop order, changed keys) and `keymap.test.ts` (the cropping keys don't clash with Develop's; both sets free of conflicts). `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (only the two existing warnings) and `npm run build` passed.

**Limitations:**
- The fitted views of the Library loupe and culling still show the camera's embedded preview, which ignores all edits (as before), so a crop appears there only at 100 % and in thumbnails.
- While straightening outside the crop tool (the Angle slider), every slider step warps the photo again at full resolution: expected to be quick on a real GPU (not measured on the owner's GPU yet) and visibly slower on the software GPU used for testing (not measured). Inside the crop tool the photo is drawn directly and nothing is warped until the crop is applied.
- The warped copy is a second full-resolution texture (8 bytes per pixel: about 190 MB for a 24 MP crop, plus a third more for its mips) while a straightened photo is open.
- At the very corners of a maximal straightened crop, the bicubic taps reach up to two pixels past the photo's edge and repeat the edge pixels (darktable's interpolation does the same).
- Lightroom's `crs:Crop*` sidecar fields are read and written since Part 7 (into sidecars that already hold Camera Raw settings). darktable doesn't see LightBench's crop unless it imports Lightroom's fields. Auto straighten comes with Upright.
- The Lightroom behaviours quoted (R, X, O in the crop tool, Ctrl-drag to straighten, the copy default, process version updates) are from general knowledge and the owner's description, not checked in Lightroom or against Adobe's pages here.
- Only one rendering exists, so the update offer was tested with the development server's simulated version 2.


## Part 2: Lens corrections

**What it does.** Corrects the lens's distortion, transverse chromatic aberration (TCA, the red and blue fringes towards the corners) and vignetting (dark corners). The data comes from the camera's own corrections when the RAW file carries them, otherwise from the Lensfun database, exactly as darktable's lens module offers them (`src/iop/lens.cc`: methods "embedded metadata" and "Lensfun database"). The correction is one more step of the single geometry resample from Part 1. A **Lens** section in the Crop & Lens group shows which data corrected the photo and with which lens, lets you pick another Lensfun camera or lens when the match is wrong, and has Lightroom-style **Distortion** and **Vignetting** amounts and a **Remove chromatic aberration** switch.

Owner's choices (2026-10-12):
1. **Data source order:** the camera's own corrections first, otherwise Lensfun (darktable's `reload_defaults` prefers the embedded data when the file has some).
2. **Lensfun database:** LightBench's own pinned copy, served with the app but not part of the install; downloaded the first time a photo needs it, then kept for offline use.
3. **On by default when a profile is found, but existing edits don't change:** an edit saved before this part keeps lens correction off and renders byte-identically; new photos and edits get Auto. One checkbox turns it off (or on, for an older edit).
4. **Show the source** (camera or Lensfun, and the lens/profile name), let the user pick another Lensfun camera or lens, and offer Distortion and Vignetting amounts and a chromatic aberration switch.

### Where the correction data comes from

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Order | The camera's data when the file has some, else Lensfun. **Use** (shown only when the file has camera data) switches a photo to Lensfun's profile instead. JPEGs aren't corrected: camera JPEGs are usually corrected in the camera, and Lensfun's profiles are made from RAW files. | Owner's decision 1; darktable `lens.cc` `reload_defaults` (`_have_embedded_metadata` → embedded method); JPEGs: LightBench's choice (darktable would correct them) |
| Camera data read | **Sony**: DistortionCorrParams 0x7037, ChromaticAberrationCorrParams 0x7035, VignettingCorrParams 0x7032 in the raw IFD (exiv2's "SubImage1"; IFD0 for DNGs darktable wrote), checked as darktable does (≤ 16 knots, CA has twice as many). **Fujifilm** (RAF): 0xf00b, 0xf00f, 0xf010 in the TIFF of the RAF's raw data (both darktable layouts: 9 and 11 knots, the knots must agree), CropMode 0x104d (1.25× crop modes). **Olympus/OM System**: 0x150a and 0x150c in the maker note's ImageProcessing IFD. **DNG**: OpcodeList3 WarpRectilinear and FixVignetteRadial. **Panasonic** RW2: DistortionInfo 0x0119. Read with LightBench's own TIFF reader (`engine/meta/exif.ts` `TiffReader`, `engine/lens/meta.ts`); LibRaw's WebAssembly binding doesn't expose these tags. When a DNG has opcodes and maker tags, the maker tags win, as in darktable. | darktable `src/common/exif.cc` `_check_lens_correction_data`, `src/common/dng_opcode.c` |
| Camera data applied | darktable's `_init_coeffs_md_v2` and `_process_md`, translated: up to 16 knots over the radius (normalised to half the diagonal, about the image centre), one factor per colour channel, a vignetting curve over the uncorrected radius, linear interpolation between knots, darktable's automatic scale folded into the knots. | darktable `lens.cc`, translated (`engine/lens/embedded.ts`) |
| Tested on real files | **Sony only** (the ILCE-6300 and ILCE-7M2 test files). Fujifilm, Olympus/OM System, DNG and Panasonic are translated from darktable and unit-tested against darktable's formulas with made-up tag data; **untested on real files** (the Lens section says “not yet checked on real files” for them). LightCraft's cautions: it trusts Sony's table only on the ILCE-7RM4A; LightBench applies it to every body that has it, as darktable does — on the A6300 file its geometry agrees with the camera's own JPEG far better than the uncorrected photo does; the A7 II file's JPEG looks uncorrected, so it confirms nothing there (Verified, below). Fujifilm: the table is read over the source radius (as darktable does) and LightCraft found the Fuji CA table made fringes worse in its pipeline; not checked here (no test file; X-Trans RAFs can't be developed in LightBench yet). | LightCraft review (`docs/research/lightcraft/CATALOG_PROCESSING.md`) |
| Nikon | Neither darktable nor LightCraft has embedded corrections for Nikon: Nikon photos (the owner's D5200, the D700 and D80 test files) use Lensfun. | darktable, LightCraft (as above) |
| Lensfun copy | `web/lensfun/db/*.xml`: Lensfun's `data/db` at commit `43f9e001ab66c4fcdd4f400463f13b72f94b2288` (2026-10-08, the newest commit when this was built; Lensfun's newest release, v0.3.4 of July 2023, carries an older database; the library code translated is from the same commit, which reads this database format, version 2), unchanged, with `COPYING.CC_BY-SA_3.0` and `source.json`. 56 files, 5.19 MB of XML (543 kB gzip). Each build converts them to one JSON file, `lensfun/lensfun-43f9e00.json` (`web/lensfun-plugin.ts`, `engine/lens/lensfun-xml.ts`): 2,022,817 bytes, 428 kB with gzip -9 (Vite's build report says 452 kB at its level), 332 kB with brotli; 52 mounts, 1,057 cameras, 1,571 lenses. JSON rather than the XML because it is less than half the size and needs no XML parser in the browser; the XML stays in the repository as the source, so the copy can be checked against Lensfun's. | Owner's decision 2; format LightBench's choice |
| Licence | Lensfun's `README.md`: “The lens database is licensed under the Creative Commons Attribution-Share Alike 3.0 license (CC BY-SA 3.0)” (text in `data/COPYING.CC_BY-SA_3.0`), confirming what distributors list. The converted JSON is an adaptation (reformatted; rows of a second model per lens and the unused ACM/none models dropped, none of which occur in this copy) and stays CC BY-SA 3.0. Credited in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and in the app (“About lens data” at the foot of the Lens section). | Lensfun repository |
| First use, offline | The service worker leaves `lensfun/` out of its precache (`sw-plugin.ts`), so installing LightBench doesn't download it. The first photo that needs Lensfun downloads it once (opening a folder doesn't); it is kept in Cache Storage under `lensfun-data` (not a `lightbench-` cache, which the service worker deletes on every update) and read from there afterwards, online or not. A newer copy has a new file name; the old one is then removed. If it can't be downloaded (offline on first use), the Lens section says so, offers **Try again**, and photos are shown uncorrected; another attempt is made after 30 s. | Owner's decision 2 (`engine/lens/lens-db.ts`) |
| Matching the camera | Lensfun's `FindCamerasExt` on the file's EXIF Make and Model: fuzzy word matching (`lfFuzzyStrCmp`: words split by character class, every word of the EXIF name must be in the database's, scored by the share of matched words), best score first. The crop factor is the Lensfun camera's, as darktable uses it. A camera not in the database gets no Lensfun correction (darktable's pipe has no crop factor then); it can be picked by hand. | Lensfun `database.cpp`, `auxfun.cpp` (translated, `engine/lens/lensfun.ts`); darktable `lens.cc` |
| Matching the lens | Lensfun's `FindLenses(camera, NULL, lens)` and `MatchScore`: crop factor against the calibration's (a profile made on a smaller sensor is refused), focal and aperture ranges guessed from the names (`GuessParameters`), the camera's mount or a compatible one, then the fuzzy name match; darktable's `_lens_sanitize` and its fixed-lens rule (shortest model name). Ties keep the database order (Lensfun's sort leaves them unspecified). | Lensfun `database.cpp`, `lens.cpp`; darktable `lens.cc` |
| The lens name | EXIF LensModel (0xa434) when the file has it (both Sony files). darktable gets lens names from exiv2's maker-note lens tables, which LightBench doesn't have. For Nikon files without LensModel (the D700 and D80 test files, and older Nikon bodies), LightBench builds a description from the unencrypted maker-note tags Lens 0x0084 and LensType 0x0083 — e.g. “18-200mm f/3.5-5.6G VR” — written the way Lensfun names Nikon lenses, so Lensfun's matching finds them; if nothing matches, it tries again without the type letters (third-party lenses). Otherwise LibRaw's lens name is used. Nikon's encrypted lens ID isn't decoded, so versions of a lens with the same specification (e.g. VR and VR II) can't be told apart (Lensfun gives these two the same calibration). | LightBench's choice (`engine/lens/meta.ts`); Nikon LensType bits as listed by exiftool (not checked against Nikon documents) |
| Shooting data | Focal length and f-number from EXIF; focus distance from EXIF SubjectDistance when present, else 1000 m (darktable's default when it has none; darktable also reads Nikon, Canon, Olympus and Sony maker-note distances, which LightBench doesn't). Without an f-number there is no vignetting correction. | darktable `lens.cc` `reload_defaults` |

### The correction

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Lensfun models, exactly | Distortion poly3, poly5 and ptlens; TCA linear and poly3; vignetting pa — evaluated as Lensfun defines them, per pixel on the GPU, never refitted to another model. Lensfun's normalised coordinates (units of the real focal length about the lens centre, `<center>` offsets included), each model's coefficients rescaled from its calibration's coordinates (`rescale_polynomial_coefficients`, including PT's focal-length-preserving 1/d), TCA applied after distortion, vignetting at the uncorrected position. The ACM models aren't implemented (the pinned database has none). | Lensfun `modifier.cpp`, `mod-coord.cpp`, `mod-subpix.cpp`, `mod-color.cpp` (LGPL-3.0, translated; `engine/lens/lensfun.ts`, `engine/gpu/warp.wgsl`) |
| Interpolation | Distortion and TCA: Lensfun's Hermite spline over the calibrated focal lengths (two neighbours each side), with Lensfun's parameter scaling by focal length; the nearest calibration outside the calibrated range. Vignetting: Lensfun's inverse distance weighting (power 3.5) over focal length (normalised to the lens's range), 4/aperture and 0.1/distance; nothing when no calibration is within distance 1. | Lensfun `lens.cpp` `InterpolateDistortion`, `InterpolateTCA`, `InterpolateVignetting`, `auxfun.cpp` `_lf_interpolate` |
| One resample | The lens function joins Part 1's map: output pixel → crop, flip, straighten (and Upright's perspective) as a 3×3 map → the lens function → sensor position, read once with bicubic interpolation (`engine/develop/geometry.ts` `withLens`, `engine/gpu/warp.wgsl`). Without lens correction the shader runs the Part 1 code unchanged. | Owner's requirement; darktable's pipe order (lens before ashift, flip and crop) |
| CA on camera RGB | darktable's lens module runs before its input colour profile, so it corrects TCA on camera RGB. LightBench's scene texture holds working-space RGB (camera RGB × the camera matrix); the warp rebuilds each camera channel from the working-space samples, reads it at its own position, and converts back. Matrix and interpolation are both linear, so this equals resampling the camera channels themselves. | darktable `src/common/iop_order.c` (lens before colorin); LightCraft review's caution |
| No empty borders | The corrected image keeps the photo's size and is enlarged just enough that no empty border shows (darktable's lens module does this by default: `reload_defaults` sets `scale = _get_autoscale_lf(...)`; for camera data, `_init_coeffs_md_v2` scales the knots). So crop coordinates (Part 1: stored in the corrected frame) stay on the same content. Lensfun's own `GetAutoScale` stops at 0.001 normalised units, a fixed share of the focal length: on the A6300 file at 97 mm it left the corners 6.6 px outside the photo. LightBench uses the same method to 1e-9 and checks 32 border points instead of 8 (wavy distortion can peak between Lensfun's eight). darktable's own search for camera data is approximate too (up to 0.17 px past the corner on the A7 II file; the edge pixel is read there). Lightroom's “Constrain crop” (shrinking the crop instead) isn't offered. | darktable `lens.cc`; the tighter search is LightBench's change, measured |
| Amounts | **Distortion** and **Vignetting**, 0–200 %, 100 = the profile. For camera data they are darktable's fine-tune factors (`cor_dist_ft`, `cor_vig_ft`). For Lensfun (which has no amounts) the same blend is applied: the radial distortion factor 1 + a·(f − 1), the vignetting falloff 1 + a·(c − 1). **Remove chromatic aberration** turns TCA off (darktable's TCA flag; for DNG warps, where darktable can't turn TCA off on its own, the channels then follow the green plane). | darktable `lens.cc` (fine-tune); for Lensfun LightBench's choice, modelled on darktable's |
| Everywhere | Develop, the histogram and Auto, Export, thumbnails, the culling/Compare/Survey 100 % renders, the background developer (Paste, Sync, presets, new photos) and virtual copies all render through the same engine path: `loadIntoEngine` gives the engine the photo's lens metadata, the lens settings travel in `displayUniforms`, and the engine resolves them (downloading Lensfun's data first when needed). The white balance picker reads through the lens function too. The crop tool shows the lens-corrected photo (a cached lens-only copy drawn through the 3×3 map, so turning the photo doesn't warp again). | Owner's requirement |

### Settings, old edits and the process version

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Stored | `lens` in the develop settings: on/off, the data source (Auto or Lensfun), a hand-picked Lensfun camera and lens with the photo they were picked for, Distortion and Vignetting amounts, CA. In the catalog, the history and the `lightbench:Develop` sidecar field. | LightBench |
| Old and new edits | A saved edit without `lens` (everything saved before this part) reads as **off**, so it renders as before (byte-identical, Verified). New edits, photos never edited and **Reset** get **Auto**: corrected when a profile is found. **Before** shows the photo as shot (the defaults without lens correction), and the Crop & Lens group's eye hides lens correction along with the crop. | Owner's decision 3 |
| Not a process version | Process versions are for the same settings rendering differently (`engine/develop/process.ts`). Here the settings themselves say whether the lens is corrected, an old edit's settings render exactly as they did, and turning lens correction on is an edit like any other (one history step) — so the process version stays 1. The Lens section notes that edits made before 0.4.0 start with it off; the checkbox turns it on. | LightBench's decision under Part 1's rules |

### The Lens section

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Where | In the Crop & Lens group (the group's heading is now “Crop & Lens”; its jump-bar label stays “Crop”), under Straighten. | USABILITY.md (Crop & Lens first) |
| Shows | “Corrected with the camera's own data (Sony).” or “Corrected with Lensfun: ⟨lens⟩ on the ⟨camera⟩.”, what it fixes (“Fixes distortion, chromatic aberration (no vignetting data).”), or why nothing was corrected (camera or lens not in Lensfun, profile made on a smaller sensor, no focal length, data couldn't be downloaded, a JPEG). | Owner's decision 4 |
| Picking | With Lensfun: **Camera** (all cameras by maker), **Make** and **Lens** (lenses that fit the camera's mount), each starting at “Auto (⟨the match⟩)”. A pick is tied to the camera and lens the file names, so it never applies to another lens (see Paste). | Lightroom's Make / Model menus (owner's description); darktable's camera and lens menus |
| Controls | Lens corrections (checkbox), Use (camera data or Lensfun, when the file has camera data), Distortion, Vignetting (sliders with hints and tooltips like the other sliders; double-click or Home resets to 100), Remove chromatic aberration. Each change is a history step. | Owner's decision 4 |

### Copy, paste and presets

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Checklist | A new **Lens** group: **Lens corrections** (on/off, the data source, a hand-picked lens), **Distortion & vignetting amounts**, **Chromatic aberration**. Unticked by default, so copying tone from an older edit doesn't turn lens correction off on new photos. | LightBench's choice |
| Re-matched per photo | The profile travels as a mode, like “As Shot”: each photo is matched with its own camera and lens. A lens picked by hand travels too but is used only on photos whose camera and lens (as their files name them) are the same as the photo it was picked on; the others match their own. | Owner's requirement |
| Presets | Presets can hold the Lens items; the Amount slider treats them as switches (they follow the preset above 0), as Part 8 does. | As Part 8 |

**Verified** (headless Chromium 141 with software WebGPU (SwiftShader); A.NEF = Nikon D700 `philadelphia.nef` (Nikkor 28-105mm at 85 mm), B.NEF = Nikon D80 `glarus.nef` (Nikkor 18-200mm VR at 18 mm), C.JPG = `test-srgb.jpg`, D.ARW = Sony ILCE-6300 `12bit-compressed-(3_2).arw` (E PZ 18-105mm F4 at 97 mm), E.ARW = Sony ILCE-7M2 `14bit-uncompressed-(3_2).arw` (E 25mm F2 at f/22); scratch scripts `e2e-lens` (23 of 23 checks passed), `e2e-measure` (17 of 17), `e2e-camjpeg` (2 of 2), `e2e-oldedit`, `e2e-offline` (5 of 5) and Part 1's `e2e-crop` (37 of 37) and `e2e-crop-extra` (9 of 9) re-run):
- **Profiles found:** A: Lensfun “Nikkor AF 28-105mm f/3.5-4.5D IF” on the Nikon D700 (lens name built from the maker note: “28-105mm f/3.5-4.5D”); B: Lensfun “Nikkor AF-S 18-200mm f/3.5-5.6G DX VR IF-ED” on the Nikon D80 (from “18-200mm f/3.5-5.6G VR”); both “Fixes distortion, chromatic aberration (no vignetting data)” — Lensfun has no vignetting data for these lenses. D and E: the camera's own data (Sony), fixing distortion, CA and vignetting; switched to Lensfun, D matched “E PZ 18-105mm f/4 G OSS on the Sony Alpha 6300” and E “Batis 25mm f/2 on the Sony Alpha 7 II” (Zeiss's Batis, which Lensfun lists under the name it writes into EXIF, “E 25mm f/2”; both lens names come from EXIF LensModel). C: “Lens corrections work on RAW photos.” A, never edited, opened with lens corrections on (Auto).
- **First use and offline:** opening the folder (thumbnails) downloaded nothing; opening A in Develop downloaded `lensfun/lensfun-43f9e00.json` once, kept in Cache Storage (`lensfun-data`, 2,022,817 bytes); B, D and E made no further request. Production build (`e2e-offline`, `vite preview`): the service worker precaches 21 files (3.56 MB), none of them Lensfun's; installing and opening a folder downloaded no lens data; B's first opening downloaded it once and corrected B; after going offline and reloading, B was corrected again from the kept copy with no request.
- **The GPU applies the models exactly** (`e2e-measure`: renders with the correction on and off at full size; seven textured patches per photo found in the corrected render by normalised cross-correlation on the green channel, compared with where the CPU translation of the model puts them): A (Lensfun) moves up to 12.36 px, worst error 0.067 px, auto-scale 1.0195; B (Lensfun) up to 40.49 px, worst 0.061 px, scale 1.0048; D camera data up to 174.04 px, worst 0.022 px; D Lensfun up to 151.97 px, worst 0.022 px, scale 1.0974; E camera data up to 18.12 px, worst 0.063 px; E Lensfun up to 15.71 px, worst 0.125 px. TCA: in every measured corner the red and blue planes moved by the model's shift to within 0.06–0.27 px (worst per run: A 0.11, B 0.15, D 0.06 and 0.13, E 0.27). On B, the D80's red fringe in the top-left corner went from 1.32, 0.87 px to 0.60, 0.44 px (Lensfun's profile moves it 0.69, 0.43); the other three corners similarly halved.
- **Vignetting** (corner patch, exposure matched between the corrected and uncorrected renders): D camera data brightened the top-left corner by +0.241 EV (the model: +0.242 EV); E camera data +0.518 EV (model +0.516); E Lensfun +1.005 EV (model +1.000; see Limitations); the centres were left alone (0 to +0.001 EV).
- **Against the camera's own JPEG** (`e2e-camjpeg`, LightCraft's method: the JPEG preview the camera embedded in the ARW, 1616 × 1080, matched patch by patch against our render at that size after one fitted scale/rotation/shift; NEF test files carry no full camera JPEG to compare with, so the Nikon files can't be checked this way): D, residual misplacement median / RMS: uncorrected 9.81 / 13.81 px, camera data 2.94 / 6.73 px, Lensfun 3.27 / 8.51 px — both corrections bring the geometry close to the camera's. E: uncorrected 0.52 / 1.22 px (maximum 8.61), camera data 1.22 / 1.19 px (maximum 1.85), Lensfun 1.90 / 2.35 px — the uncorrected render matches best by median, so this camera JPEG was probably not distortion-corrected in the camera (the body's distortion setting wasn't read; inconclusive for E).
- **Old edits unchanged** (`e2e-oldedit`): with the `lightbench/crop` code (Part 1), A was edited (Exposure +0.5, Contrast +20, Shadows +10, Saturation −10, straightened 1.35°), its `A.xmp` kept, B and C left unedited; the folder was then opened with this part's code. A, B and C read as lens corrections off (A's Lens section: “Off: the photo is shown as the lens drew it.”, checkbox unticked); full-size exports had identical hashes (A 4136 × 2746: `979e8ec165bc7e90…` both times), the 1024 px exports and the thumbnails were byte-identical (0 bytes of 2,785,280 and 696,320 differ for A), the histogram statistics identical (43,520 pixels, mean luma 120.2262), and so was A's Develop canvas (890 × 662, 0 bytes differ). B with the new defaults (Auto) does change (1,510,459 bytes of its 1024 px export differ), as expected.
- **Checkbox and Before:** on D, unticking recorded “Lens corrections off”; the picture then matched Before exactly (outside the Before badge); ticking again gave back the same picture byte for byte; the correction changed the picture visibly (mean difference 9.3 levels).
- **Everything renders it:** D's thumbnail path and export path at 512 × 342 differed by 2.1 levels on average with the correction on, against 13.58 between the export and the uncorrected thumbnail; the histogram statistics changed with it (mean 113.89 corrected, 108.67 not). The crop tool opened E on the full 6024 × 4024 frame and showed D lens-corrected with no empty border (screenshot). Part 1's crop tests passed unchanged apart from the group's new name (“Crop & Lens”); with lens correction on, the crop tool appears after the lens-only copy is warped (6.1 s for the D700 file on the software GPU).
- **Picking and paste:** on A, picking “Nikkor AF 50mm f/1.8D” showed “Corrected with Lensfun: Nikkor AF 50mm f/1.8D on the Nikon D700.” (the full-frame calibration, not the APS-C one listed under the same name). The Lens items were unticked in the Copy checklist; with them ticked and Distortion 80, pasting onto B gave B Distortion 80 while B stayed “Corrected with Lensfun: Nikkor AF-S 18-200mm …” (the 50 mm pick was tied to A's camera and lens); B.xmp held the lens settings.
- Screenshots of the Lens section (A Lensfun with the pickers, D camera data, D switched to Lensfun, A with a picked lens and the Distortion tooltip), D before/after and D in the crop tool were checked by eye.
- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`, 388 passing; 59 new in `engine/lens`): `lensfun.test.ts` (23: the three distortion models, TCA and vignetting against reference functions written from Hugin's/Lensfun's formulas on real database entries, interpolation between calibrations, fuzzy word matching, camera and lens matching on real entries with Lensfun's scores, `GuessParameters`, auto-scale), `embedded.test.ts` (12: darktable's Sony, Fujifilm, DNG, Olympus and Panasonic coefficient set-up, including the real ILCE-7M2 tags), `meta.test.ts` (8: synthetic TIFF, RAF, ORF, RW2, DNG and Nikon maker-note files), `correction.test.ts` (16: old and new defaults, source choice, reasons, picks tied to their photo, duplicate calibrations by crop, the warp key, the shader's uniform layout, paste).

**Limitations:**
- Sony's CA tables don't always agree with the photos: on D the red fringe in the bottom corners (1.4 px) was moved 2.1 px, leaving 0.6 px the other way; on E the top corners' blue fringe went from about 1.0 px to 0.4 px but the bottom corners' small fringes grew to about 0.6 px. The shader applies the table as darktable reads it (measured to 0.06–0.27 px). Part 9 found why: LightBench reads the table exactly as darktable does; the RCD demosaic, which runs before the correction (as in darktable), already hides 37–50 % of D's CA, so the full correction overshoots (with bilinear demosaicing 80–95 % of the table's CA is visible and 0.04–0.37 px remain); E's CA isn't radially symmetric, which no radial table can fix. Not changed (darktable behaves the same). **Remove chromatic aberration** turns it off.
- Only Sony's camera data was tested on real files (two bodies). Fujifilm, Olympus/OM System, DNG and Panasonic follow darktable's code and unit tests with made-up data. LibRaw's active area is used as the image (darktable uses rawspeed's crop: for the two Sony files 6020 and 6022 px wide against LibRaw's 6024, so darktable's centre is 1–2 px further left; Part 9: at most 0.002 px of CA and about 0.3 px of distortion at the corners).
- Where Sony's distortion knots lie along the radius is unresolved: darktable (and LightBench) put them at (i + 0.5)/(nc − 1); LightCraft found i/(nc − 1) on another body; D's camera JPEG agrees better with either or stronger readings (Part 9). Not changed.
- The Nikon lens description can't tell versions of a lens with the same specification apart, or a third-party lens from a Nikon one with the same specification; the Lens section shows the match and the lens can be picked by hand.
- The focus distance comes only from EXIF SubjectDistance (else 1000 m); vignetting profiles that depend on distance use the far calibration then.
- Lensfun interpolates vignetting beyond its calibrations: the Batis 25 mm profile has apertures up to f/4, so the A7 II file at f/22 gets f/4-like corner brightening (+1.0 EV against the camera data's +0.52 EV). This is Lensfun's (and darktable's) behaviour; the Vignetting amount can lower it.
- Fisheye lenses are corrected within their own projection (darktable's default target geometry is the lens's own type); conversion to rectilinear isn't offered.
- ~~The sensor-clipping overlay maps through the crop and straighten but not the lens function, so with lens correction on it can sit a few pixels off near the corners.~~ Fixed in Part 9: the overlay reads the sensor through the same lens function as the warp (on the A7 II file it had been 15–17 px off at 100 % in the top corners, the lens correction's shift there).
- Every lens-corrected photo is warped once at full resolution when opened (like a straightened photo in Part 1), and the warped copy is kept (about 190 MB for 24 MP).
- The Lensfun data needs one download (about 0.43 MB compressed) before Nikon and other photos without camera data are corrected; offline on first use they are shown uncorrected, with a note.

## Part 3: Transform and Upright

**What it does.** A **Transform** section in the Crop group corrects perspective: leaning buildings, walls seen at an angle and tilted horizons. **Upright** does it in one click (Auto, Level, Vertical, Full) or from lines drawn on the photo (Guided); Lightroom's **Transform sliders** adjust it by hand. The crop group's Straighten controls get an **Auto** button that levels the photo along the lines it finds. The engine is darktable's "rotate and perspective" module (ashift): its line detector, its choice and weighting of lines, its fit with the photo's own focal length, and its automatic crop; LightCraft's guards keep it from "correcting" trees, roads and other scene structure.

Owner's choices (2026-10-12):
1. **All four Upright ways:** Auto (level and verticals in one click), Level, Vertical and Full as separate one-click fixes, Guided (draw 2–4 lines), and the Manual sliders (Vertical, Horizontal, Rotate, Aspect, Scale, X/Y Offset).
2. **Auto straighten** in the crop group, sharing the line detector, setting the existing straighten Angle.
3. **darktable's ashift as the engine**, translated to TypeScript, with the photo's real focal length and crop factor; **LightCraft's guards** against false corrections; the **solved correction stored in the edit**; Auto re-detected per photo when pasted, Manual values copied; no empty corners after the transform; existing edits unchanged.

### Upright

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Modes | **Off**, **Auto**, **Guided**, **Level**, **Vertical**, **Full**: buttons at the top of Transform. Each click is one history step (“Upright: Vertical”); a refusal changes nothing and says why under the buttons. | Lightroom's Upright modes (names from general knowledge of Lightroom; not checked in Lightroom here) |
| Level | Rotation only, from the lines that are horizontal on screen when there are at least two, else from the vertical ones (both when both). | darktable's rotation-only fits (ctrl-click on ashift's fit buttons: `ASHIFT_FIT_ROTATION_*_LINES`); choosing the lines by what is horizontal on screen is LightBench's |
| Vertical | Rotation and vertical lens shift from the vertical lines (`ASHIFT_FIT_VERTICALLY`). | darktable ashift |
| Full | Rotation, both lens shifts and shear from both families (`ASHIFT_FIT_BOTH_SHEAR`, ashift's default “fit both”). Without reliable horizontals, only the verticals are corrected, and it says so. | darktable ashift; the fallback is LightCraft's (`solve`, `Upright::Full`) |
| Auto | LightCraft's balanced policy on darktable's fits: a roll implied by the verticals must be confirmed by the horizontals (within 3°), else the verticals are ignored; then Full if the correction is under 20°, else Vertical if under 25°, else Level if it turns the photo 5° or less; otherwise nothing, with a message. The size of a correction is darktable's rotation combined with the tilts its lens shifts stand for (`atan(rad_v)`, `atan(rad_h)` in `_homography`), in degrees. | LightCraft `upright.rs` (`solve`, `Upright::Auto`); measuring it on ashift's model is LightBench's |
| Upright replaces Straighten | Applying a mode sets the straighten Angle to 0 (the crop is kept, shrunk if needed), so the two rotations never add up. | LightBench's choice (Lightroom resets the crop's angle too, as far as known; not checked) |
| Shown | Under the buttons: what the correction does (“Upright: turned 0.0°, vertical tilt 11.5° (36 mm equivalent)”), a refusal or a note in amber, and “No focal length in the file: assuming 28 mm.” when the file has none. | LightBench |

### Line detection, the fit and the guards

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Detector | LSD 1.6 (Grompone von Gioi et al.) with darktable's parameters: scale 0.99, sigma 0.6, quantisation 2, angle tolerance 22.5°, log ε 0, density 0.7, 1024 bins. Input: grey = 0.3 R + 0.59 G + 0.11 B of the scene-linear photo raised to 0.45, × 256. Runs in a worker. | darktable `ashift_lsd.c`, `line_detect`, `rgb2grey256`, `gamma_correct`; darktable applies the gamma to RAW input only, LightBench to JPEGs too because its JPEGs are scene-linear as well |
| Input image | The photo before the perspective, at most 1600 px on its long edge (a level of the scene's mip chain, so nothing is rendered for it), detected once per photo. With lens correction on (Part 2), it is the lens-corrected photo, as darktable's ashift sits after `lens` (`DevelopEngine.analysisImage` reads the engine's lens-only corrected scene; Develop detects again when the lens correction changes). | darktable's pipe order; the 1600 px limit is LightBench's |
| Line selection | Lines along the image border dropped; weight = length × width × precision; within 30° of vertical or horizontal and longer than 5 px; then darktable's RANSAC keeps the lines meeting in one vanishing point per direction (self-tuning ε, 400 runs, full permutations for ≤ 5 lines; vanishing points inside the photo rejected). | darktable `line_detect`, `ransac`, `_remove_outliers`; a seeded generator replaces C's `rand()` so a photo always gives the same result |
| Model and fit | darktable's `_homography` (rotation, shear, vertical and horizontal lens shift with their compression, aspect) and Nelder-Mead fit of `model_fitness` (400 iterations, ε 10⁻³; parameters through darktable's logit with its “soft” ranges ±180°, ±2, ±0.5). Fits that more than quadruple the image area are refused (`NMS_INSANE`). | darktable `_homography`, `model_fitness`, `nmsfit`, `ashift_nmsimplex.c` (Michael F. Hutt, MIT) |
| Focal length | The EXIF focal length × crop factor (35 mm FocalLengthIn35mmFilm / focal length, or 1), as darktable's `reload_defaults` reads them, used with ashift's “specific” lens model and its default lens dependence of 100 %. 28 mm when the file has none. Stored with the correction. | darktable `reload_defaults`, `DEFAULT_F_LENGTH`; darktable's own default is the “generic” model (28 mm, no lens dependence); the owner asked for the real focal length |
| Units | Detection, fit and rendering all use pixels of the full-size photo in its sensor orientation. darktable fits on its preview buffer's size while its lines are scaled to full size (`nmsfit`: `fit.width = g->lines_in_width`); LightBench keeps one size so the result renders as fitted. | Reading of `nmsfit` (not run in darktable here) |
| Guards | A family's vanishing point counts only with at least 3 inlier lines, a total inlier length of ¼ of the long edge, inliers spread across ⅕ of it, 60 % of the family's length agreeing, and a vanishing point at least one long edge away from the photo's centre (closer, it is a road or sun rays, not camera tilt). Level and Auto straighten need half of the lines' length to agree within 2° (else “The lines found don’t agree on a level”). Fewer than 2 lines: “Not enough straight lines were found to correct this photo.” | LightCraft `reliable`, `level_angle`; darktable `MINIMUM_FITLINES` = 2 |
| Guided | Draw 2–4 lines on the photo (Shift+T or the Guided button). The photo is shown whole, corrected, with the part beyond its frame dimmed; drag a line's ends to adjust it, click it and press Delete (or double-click it) to remove it, Esc or Enter to finish. Two or more lines closer to vertical fix the verticals (`ASHIFT_FIT_VERTICALLY`), two or more horizontal ones the horizontals (`ASHIFT_FIT_HORIZONTALLY`), both kinds everything with shear; with fewer, the photo stays uncorrected and the hint says “Draw one more line in the same direction.” Lines are darktable's drawn lines (weight 1, type from their direction). While a line is drawn or dragged the photo doesn't move; it is solved again on release. Each add, move and removal is a history step. | darktable's drawn-lines method (`_draw_basic_line`, `_draw_retrieve_line_type`, `MINIMUM_FITLINES`); four lines at most is Lightroom's as the owner described it; the on-photo behaviour is LightBench's |

### Transform sliders

| Slider | Range | What it does (darktable parameter) |
|---|---|---|
| Vertical | −100…100 | ashift's vertical lens shift (±100 = ±1, darktable's `LENSSHIFT_RANGE`); + widens the top |
| Horizontal | −100…100 | horizontal lens shift; + narrows the right side |
| Rotate | −10…10° | ashift's rotation (`ROTATION_RANGE` 10); + turns clockwise |
| Aspect | −100…100 | ashift's aspect adjust, 2^(value/100) (darktable's 0.5…2 range); + wider |
| Scale | 100…150 % | zooms into the fitted photo (100 % = largest without empty corners) |
| X / Y Offset | −100…100 | moves the photo inside its frame (±100 = a quarter of the photo); the frame shrinks where needed. X + moves it right, Y + up |

The sliders add to what Upright solved (they are stored apart, as in Lightroom). Directions, the Scale range and the offset scale are LightBench's choices (Lightroom's own ranges and directions are from general knowledge, not checked). Each slider has the usual hint and tooltip.

### Stored transform, geometry and the crop

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| What is stored | `geometry.transform` in the develop settings (catalog, history, `lightbench:Develop` sidecar): the Upright mode, **the solved correction** (darktable's rotation, lens shifts and shear), the guides, the slider values and the focal length used. Rendering uses these numbers and never detects lines again, so Develop, Export, thumbnails, the 100 % renderer, the background developer and virtual copies render the same. An edit without it has no transform. | Owner's requirement; LightCraft stores its homography (`Geometry::upright_transform`); darktable stores the fitted parameters |
| Where it sits | Lens correction → **perspective** → straighten → flip → crop (darktable: `lens` → `ashift` → `flip` → `crop`). The perspective is the 3×3 map `perspectiveMap` inserted into `resolveGeometry`'s single map, before straightening, in the sensor's orientation as in darktable, so it stays on the content when the photo is turned or flipped. | darktable `iop_order.c`; Part 1's geometry pass |
| Screen terms | Settings are stored as seen on screen (Vertical is vertical on screen, whatever the camera's orientation) and converted to the sensor's axes when rendering. Rotate and flip convert them too (`turnTransform`), so the correction stays on the content; a unit test checks the map is unchanged for every orientation and turn. | darktable relabels its sliders for sideways photos (`isflipped`); storing screen terms is LightBench's choice, so pasting between differently oriented photos means the same thing |
| No empty corners | The corrected photo is fitted into its frame (the photo's own size) with darktable's automatic crop in “original format” mode: the largest rectangle of the photo's shape inside the corrected outline, its centre found by Nelder-Mead (`do_crop`, `crop_fitness`, `ASHIFT_CROP_ASPECT`). Scale and the offsets act on that fit. So the frame never shows empty areas, and the crop (stored in that frame, as in Part 1) stays inside it. Lightroom's Constrain Crop checkbox is not offered: it is always on (showing empty areas would need the warp to paint outside the photo). | darktable ashift (crop mode verified in `ashift.c`); always on is LightBench's choice |
| Resample | A transform makes the map non-exact: the photo is warped once (bicubic) and cached, as with a straighten angle. The crop tool's view shows the corrected photo directly. | Part 1 |
| Crop eye | The Crop group's eye now also hides the transform. | LightBench |
| Process version | Unchanged (still 1): a new tool that is off by default needs no new version (the rule in `process.ts`). A later change to the transform's maths would. | Part 1's rule |

### Auto straighten

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Where | **Auto** next to Level in Straighten; with the Level tool on, a **double-click** on the photo does the same. Works while cropping (a step of the crop session) and outside it (history step “Auto straighten”). | Owner's request; double-click for Auto is LightCraft's idea (`straighten_overlay`), LightBench's choice to include it |
| How | The detected lines are mapped through the current transform into the frame as seen, darktable's outlier removal and a rotation-only fit find the angle, and LightCraft's level consensus must agree; the Angle is set through Part 1's `setAngle` (the crop shrinks if needed). Over 45° or no agreement: nothing changes and it says why. | darktable ashift; LightCraft `level_degrees` |

### Copy, paste and presets

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Checklist | Two new items in the Crop group of the Copy/Sync/Paste/New preset checklist, unticked by default like the crop: **Upright** — shown as e.g. “Auto (detected again on each photo)” or “Guided (its correction is copied as it is)” — and **Transform sliders** — “Vertical +10 (copied as they are)”. | Owner's requirement (Auto re-runs per photo, like Auto tone) |
| Re-detection | Auto, Level, Vertical and Full are solved again on each photo after pasting, in Develop and in the background developer (Paste on a selection, Sync, Auto Sync) and for presets; a photo where nothing reliable is found keeps the mode with no correction. Guided copies its solved correction (guides belong to one photo). The sliders are copied; each photo's own focal length is used. | LightBench |

### Shortcuts added

| Action | Where | Key |
|---|---|---|
| Transform: Guided Upright | Develop, and again to finish | Shift+T (LightBench's choice; Lightroom Classic's key for its Guided Upright tool not checked here) |
| Finish Guided Upright / Remove the selected guide | Develop: Guided Upright | Enter, Esc / Delete, Backspace |
| Undo / Redo | also while drawing guides | Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y, Ctrl+Shift+Z |
| Straighten: Auto; Transform: Upright Auto; Upright off; reset Upright and the sliders | Develop (Auto straighten also while cropping) | no key (command search) |

A new registry context, **Develop: Guided Upright** (`guided`), is active instead of Develop while drawing guides, so Delete and Esc act on the guides; the keymap test covers it.

**Verified** (headless Chromium 141 with software WebGPU (SwiftShader); A.NEF = Nikon D700 `philadelphia.nef` (85 mm), B.NEF = Nikon D80 `glarus.nef`, C.JPG = `test-srgb.jpg`, D.ARW = Sony ILCE-7M2 `14bit-uncompressed-(3_2).arw` (bare trees); synthetic JPEGs made for this part: K = a facade shot with the camera tilted up 12° at 36 mm-equivalent (EXIF focal length 36 mm), T = a sea horizon turned 3° clockwise, F = a featureless gradient; scratch scripts `e2e-upright` (50 of 50 checks passed), `e2e-oldedit` and Part 1's `e2e-crop`. “Tilt” is the length-weighted mean angle of the near-vertical lines from vertical, measured on the rendered image with LightBench's own detector):
- **Old edits unchanged** (`e2e-oldedit`): an edit made with the code before this part (`lightbench/crop` at `6cabdeb`; Exposure +0.5, Contrast +20, Shadows +10, Saturation −10, straighten +4.5° and a turn right on A) was saved, its `A.xmp` kept, and the folder opened again with this part's code. Full-size exports of A (edited), B and C (unedited) had identical SHA-256 hashes before and after; 1024 px exports and 512 px thumbnails differed in 0 bytes; histogram statistics were identical (A: 43,520 pixels, mean luma 120.331); the Develop canvas screenshot (890 × 662) differed in 0 bytes. Unit tests: an edit without a transform normalises to exactly Part 1's settings (no `transform` key) and resolves to exactly the same map for all four camera orientations.
- **Transform section** (`transform-section.png`): six Upright buttons and the seven sliders under Straighten in the Crop group; K's focal length (36 mm) was read from its EXIF, C.JPG (no EXIF focal length) showed “assuming 28 mm”, and the RAWs' focal lengths were read (A 85 mm, B 27 mm = 18 mm × 1.5, D known).
- **Upright on K** (converging verticals measured 2.58° from vertical before): **Vertical** solved rotation −0.009°, vertical lens shift 0.1358 (a tilt of 11.5° at 36 mm; the photo was made with a 12° tilt), verticals 0.01° after; **Full** the same plus horizontal shift 0.0005, shear 0; **Auto** chose the Full result (under 20°), 0.01° after; **Level** refused (“The lines found don’t agree on a level”: converging verticals are not a level) and left the photo as it was. Each was one history step (“Upright: Vertical”) and left the straighten angle at 0.
- **Upright Level and Auto straighten on T** (horizon turned 3° clockwise): Level solved rotation 3.0003°, the horizon lines measured 0.00° after; **Auto straighten** set the Angle to −3.00° (history step “Auto straighten”, horizon 0.00° after); in the crop tool, with Level on, a double-click set −3.00° as well.
- **Guards:** the featureless F was refused by Upright Auto and Auto straighten (“Not enough straight lines were found to correct this photo.”), no history step. On the real photos, which have few straight edges, the guards held back: A (philadelphia.nef, a fountain among trees, 85 mm): Auto and Level turned it 0.85° clockwise (from the basin and path edges; no ground truth, not judged), Vertical refused (“The vertical lines found don’t meet in one point outside the photo”), Full refused; B (glarus.nef, mountains and lake) and C (test-srgb.jpg): every mode refused (no agreeing lines, unreliable verticals); D (bare trees, the false-correction case LightCraft's guards target): every mode refused, so the trunks were not “corrected”.
- **Guided on K** (`guided-lines.png`): Shift+T opened it with the on-photo hint and the part beyond the frame dimmed; one line drawn along a pilaster asked for “one more line”; a second made both vertical on screen (x equal to 0.1 px; solved vertical shift 0.1253, i.e. 10.6°) and the pilasters measured 2.58° → 0.18° after; dragging an end re-solved (step “Guided Upright: move line”); click + Delete removed a line (back to “Draw one more line”), Ctrl+Z restored it; Esc finished with Guided still on and the view still fitted.
- **Manual sliders:** Shift+→ on Vertical (×2), Horizontal, Rotate, Aspect and Scale gave Vertical 20, Horizontal 10, Rotate +1.0°, Aspect 10, Scale 110 % on top of Auto's result, five history steps (“Transform: Vertical” …), and changed the photo. Rotate right afterwards kept the same map on the sensor (sliders read Vertical 10, Horizontal −20, Aspect −10 in the new orientation); Reset recorded “Reset transform”.
- **No empty corners:** with an X offset of +40, every output corner of the frame, and of a crop turned 8°, mapped inside the 1800 × 1200 photo (corners from (0, 120) to (1440, 1080) and (157, 251) to (1313, 795)); unit tests check the same for every camera orientation, flips, turns, a crop and a 7° angle.
- **Export and other renderings** (Vertical on K): the PNG export at 100 % was 1800 × 1200 (the crop's size); resized to the Develop view's size it differed from the view by a mean of 1.55 levels (max 92, at the edges of the high-contrast pilasters, as in Part 1); verticals 0.01° in both. The library thumbnail (0.00°), the 100 % renderer used by culling, Compare and Survey (0.01°) and a virtual copy (0.01°) showed the correction.
- **Paste** (`paste-checklist.png`): in the Copy checklist, Upright and Transform sliders were unticked by default and read “Auto (detected again on each photo)” and “Vertical +10 (copied as they are)”. Pasted in Develop onto A: Upright Auto was detected again on A (its own result, rotation −0.85°, with A's 85 mm) and Vertical +10 copied. Pasted in the Library onto B (background developer, “1 photo updated”): Auto detected again (nothing reliable: no correction, mode kept) and Vertical +10 copied, with B's 27 mm.
- **Part 1's crop script re-run** (`e2e-crop`): 37 of 37 checks passed (it now finds Basic's Auto by its exact name; the new Auto buttons are named “Auto straighten” and “Upright: Auto” for screen readers and scripts).
- Screenshots of the Transform section, its sliders with a tooltip, Guided with two lines, the before/after of K, the guard message and the paste checklist were checked by eye.
- **Unit tests** (`npx vitest run`, 353 passing): `upright.test.ts` (the simplex finds a known minimum; LSD finds drawn lines on a synthetic image within 30 px and nothing on flat or gradient images; Vertical, Full and Level recover known darktable parameters from lines seen through them (rotation within 0.05–0.1°, lens shifts within 0.01); sideways photos fit in screen terms; Auto refuses a large correction; featureless photos and lines meeting inside the photo are refused; detector + fit + map straighten a synthetic keystone to under 0.5°; Guided needs two lines a direction and makes two guides vertical; guide points round-trip; old edits; broken values; screen ⇄ sensor parameters round-trip and a turn keeps the same sensor map for all 8 × 8 orientation pairs; rotate and flip keep the transform on the content; no empty corners with crop, angle, flips and every camera orientation; Auto straighten finds −4° on a 4° tilt and refuses no lines) and `paste.test.ts` (Upright and the sliders unticked by default; Auto travels as a mode without numbers; Guided copies its result; sliders copied; Auto Sync doesn't resend Upright for other changes). `keymap.test.ts` covers the new Guided Upright context. `npx tsc -b`, `npx oxlint` (only the two existing warnings) and `npm run build` passed (the line-detection worker is precached for offline use).
- Not reproduced: in one full run the first photo opened (K) rendered black on the software GPU while the library was still making thumbnails of a 24 MP RAW; three later runs (with a 15 s pause after opening the folder) rendered it normally. Not investigated further; it may also happen without this part.

**Limitations:**
- The test photos have few strong verticals: philadelphia.nef is mostly trees and a fountain, so Upright's vertical correction was checked on synthetic photos and Guided lines, not on a real building. Behaviour on the owner's own architecture photos is not verified.
- Lightroom's Upright, Transform and Guided behaviours and keys quoted here are from general knowledge and the owner's description, not checked in Lightroom.
- Constrain Crop is always on; Lightroom's option to see the empty areas is not offered.
- Lightroom's Upright mode and Transform sliders (`crs:PerspectiveUpright`, `crs:Perspective*`) are read and written since Part 7 (a Lightroom Upright is solved again by LightBench's own); Lightroom's solved Upright (`crs:Upright*` matrices, centre, focal mode, guide lines) is not read or written. LightBench's own transform lives in `lightbench:Develop`.
- Each Transform slider step warps the photo again at full resolution (as the Angle slider does, Part 1); quick on a real GPU is expected, not measured on the owner's GPU.
- Line detection runs on the CPU in a worker: about 0.3 s for a 900 × 600 synthetic image in Node (unit test probe); in the browser it was not timed separately.
- LSD is AGPL-3.0-or-later (see THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md): its source-offer terms apply to the web app; the app links to its source code from Settings and System Check (the owner's decision).
- Lines are detected on the lens-corrected photo when lens correction is on (added when Parts 2 and 3 were merged); that path was checked by unit tests and the build, not by an e2e run of Upright on a lens-corrected photo.

## Part 4: Extras — twin sidecars, preview colour, GPS, IPTC, Display P3, JPEG Tint

**What it does.** Five small things the owner chose from the LightCraft review (the “extras bundle”, [research/LIGHTCRAFT.md](research/LIGHTCRAFT.md)), plus the Tint bug found in Phase 2. There is nothing new to learn and no setting was added: a JPEG shot next to a RAW keeps its own sidecar; RAWs shot in Adobe RGB get correct thumbnails; photos show where they were taken; IPTC captions and keywords from older apps appear; wide-gamut screens show wide-gamut colour in Develop; extreme Tint on a JPEG no longer inverts its colours.

### Own sidecar for RAW+JPEG twins

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| The rule | Of the files sharing a base name in a folder, the RAW owns Lightroom-style `NAME.xmp`; every other file uses darktable-style `NAME.ext.xmp` (`IMG_1.JPG.xmp`). With no RAW (or two), the first by file name, ignoring case, owns it, so it never depends on the order photos were added. A file alone keeps `NAME.xmp`, as before. `web/src/sidecar/naming.ts`. | LightCraft's rule (`crates/engine/src/sidecar.rs` lines 15–18, `StemOwners` line 490, `sidecar_naming` line 534; `docs/xmp-interop.md` “Shared names”) |
| Why | Adobe names a sidecar after the file with its extension replaced by `.xmp` (XMP Specification Part 3, 2020, p. 7: “a file with the same name as the main document but with an .xmp extension”), which is what Lightroom and Bridge read for the RAW. Adobe keeps a JPEG's settings inside the JPEG, so it has no name for a JPEG twin's sidecar (Adobe's Camera Raw help says TIFF and JPEG settings are stored in the file itself; seen only in a search summary, the page couldn't be opened from here: *unverified*). darktable always writes `NAME.ext.xmp` for every file (`src/common/image.c`, `dt_image_write_sidecar_file` lines 3109–3111). So the RAW keeps the name Adobe's apps read, and the JPEG gets the name darktable uses for it. | As listed |
| Before | A JPEG next to its RAW wrote no sidecar at all (`owner` false in `sidecarSource`), so its stars, keywords and edits lived only in the catalog. | LightBench 0.2 |
| Writing | The JPEG twin writes `IMG_1.JPG.xmp`, merging into an existing one (darktable's own fields in it are kept). The RAW still never writes into darktable's `NAME.NEF.xmp`. | LightBench |
| Reading when added | The RAW reads `NAME.xmp`, else `NAME.NEF.xmp` (as before). The JPEG twin reads its own `NAME.JPG.xmp`; without one it borrows the RAW's `NAME.xmp` (as LightBench always did, and as LightCraft does) but takes only stars, flag, label, keywords and metadata, not the RAW's edits, virtual copies, stack or collections. A JPEG alone still reads the `NAME.xmp` an older LightBench wrote for it. | LightCraft (`find_sidecar` falls back to the stem name); limiting a borrowed file is LightBench's choice |
| Change detection | Each twin watches its own file: the folder check, the Loupe/Develop check, Read changes and Ignore look at `NAME.xmp` for the RAW and `NAME.JPG.xmp` for the JPEG (`sidecarStates` now returns every original with an `owner` flag). | LightBench |

### Correct colours for Adobe RGB camera previews

| Behaviour | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Which previews | The RAW says its embedded JPEG is Adobe RGB when the Nikon maker note's ColorSpace (`0x001e`) is 2, or (never for a DNG) its EXIF ColorSpace is `0xFFFF` with InteropIndex `R03`.
